- This apporach is loop 1 to make hash table -> O(N)
- Loop 2 with O(N)
- Nested while loop which at worst can go for O(N) 
Finally O(N) + O(N^2) = O(N^2) for Time Complexity

- For space we are creating a new hash table with constant integers as key and pairs so O(N)
while retruning a variable which is of constant length so overall O(N) auxillary space   ^vxZC4yMe

i = 0 ^RlEwlD8I

To optimize brute force we need to find a way to know if we need to go over sequences like 4, 5. 3,4,5 etc since we already go over them in 2.
- There is one gotcha here that if i see 3 as starting point i can check in hash table if i have already seen 2 that would mean i should ignore sine 3 would be part of sequence in 2 offcourse.  ^2s1vs485

- So ideally what makes a number good starting point is that it has no previous
number avaialble like in case of 2 we have no value as 1 while we have 3 
as next number available ^DuIuKLHW

if nums[0] - 1 is not in hashTable and nums[0] + 1 in hashTable {
    we should count it as good starting point
}else if nums[0] - 1 is in hashTable it is not good starting point for sequence ^e32cBfGl

def longestConsecutive(nums: List[integer]) returns integer {
    hashTable = {}
    // make a hash table
    for value in nums {
        if value is not in hashTable {
            hashTable[value] = 1  
        }else {
            hashTable[value] = hashTable[value] + 1
        }
    }

    // Loop through each element
    longestConsecutiveSequence = 0
    for i in range(0,nums.length) {
        if nums[i] - 1 not in hashTable {
            count = 1
            curentValue = nums[i]
            while currentValue+1 in hashTable {
                count = count + 1
                currentValue = currentValue + 1
            }
            if count is gretaer than longestConsecutiveSequence {
                longestConsecutiveSequence = count
            }
        }
    }
    return longestConsecutiveSequence
}  ^1xTmimFV

O(2N) ^JT2280fy

- Time complexty for one looo 1 -> O(N)
for second loop O(2N) since if at worst case like [0,3,5,7,10] we will visit each one twice.
Total Time Complexity = O(N) + O(2N) = O(N) 

- Space Complexity =>
HashTable since it store integers so O(N),
then individual variable are constants O(1) so finally O(N)  ^ZxFRyRO7

def longestConsecutive(nums: List[integer]) returns integer {
    set = new Set(nums)

    // Loop through each element
    longestConsecutiveSequence = 0
    for i in range(0,set.length) {
        if nums[i] - 1 not in set {
            count = 1
            curentValue = nums[i]
            while currentValue+1 in set {
                count = count + 1
                currentValue = currentValue + 1
            }
            if count is gretaer than longestConsecutiveSequence {
                longestConsecutiveSequence = count
            }
        }
    }
    return longestConsecutiveSequence
}  ^ZdjV9xzh

This approach loops over set instead of nums which removes the chance to go over duplciate goood starting ponts overall ^1mItKKE3

with Set ^dFJev4Ls
